# Step 3, part 10: The pull-request stack, contracts and docs

Part of [step 3's plan](../3-expressions.md) ("Expressions"), which holds the baseline, the
decisions and "Written and not yet run". Citations are `path::symbol` for Nova at `main` `5cf6e327`. This part makes no
CommCare claim of its own: every CommCare fact it relies on is a fact of another part, cited there at
the pins `proof/pins.json` names (commcare-hq `d6c6e16d8ae1`, commcare-core `8e9ba8d908e9`,
commcare-android `7a5584475580`, formplayer `24383ac71bfb`, commcare-connect `046c7fd78081`, Vellum
`01215f251c57`).

This part owns no finding, so it carries no Today / Fix block of its own. It orders the work parts 01
to 09 plan into one stack, gives the checklist every model addition follows, and lists every contract
sentence, public docs page and sibling plan step 3 rewrites. Where a pull request is named by number
anywhere in this plan, the number is the one in the table below. Every cutover step id, notice
reason and blocker code here is the one part 09 registers (§5, §6, §11, §12); that registry is the one
list, and this part only says which pull request's behavior each one reports.

The stack is cut from the latest `origin/main` after this plan's own pull request merges (the plan
pull request, as #727 was step 2's). That pull request also writes the deferral notes into the
outline files of the steps that receive them, and re-points the two citations of the outline it
replaces ("Written by this plan's own pull request"). Issue 768's
fix is #771, stacked on the plan's pull request (house rule 8). Counts are of `main` at `5cf6e327`, read for this part:
`proof/known-defects.json` 282 entries, `proof/fixed-defects.json` 720, `proof/walk-stops.json` 29,
`proof/controls/` 105 directories, `proof/rules/__init__.py::RULES` 25 spelling rules,
`proof/checks/registers.py::OPEN_DEFECTS` `{20: 4, 21: 5, 23: 5, 24: 5, 25: 5, 26: 5, 27: 5, 28: 5,
30: 5, 56: 3}`, `lib/models.ts::MODEL_CONTEXT_VERSION` `"v3-hq-round-trip-emission.14"`,
`lib/db/appTests.ts::RUNTIME_VERSION` 15, and `scripts/test-schema.ts::SCHEMA_TESTS` 48 entries, all
48 named in its header's "Known names".

## The stack

One `gh stack` chain of fifteen pull requests, merged together for one deploy. Each row's "Green
alone" is in addition to the repo's ordinary checks at that pull request's own head: typecheck,
lint, Vitest with Postgres, the production build, the pull request lane in CI, the Playwright smoke
and the Python infra tests. No row runs a served reader on this machine. "Register" counts entries
by id: "adds" are entries the pull request registers live, "moves" are entries that leave
`proof/known-defects.json` for `proof/fixed-defects.json`. A gate change moves only the exact entry
whose registered symptom it removes from the admitted corpus, with only `document` dropped, when
the unchanged retained control still shows that symptom; other entries of the same defect stay
live (`proof/README.md`, "Moving an entry when its defect is fixed"). "Lane" is the row of part 08 §6.1 the
pull request's lane runs and its expected end: a stacked pull request never reads its base's records
(`proof/ci/store-keys.mjs`), so pull requests 2 and 3, which give the lane no input of their own,
still run pull request 1's 10.2-minute row, and from pull request 4, which moves most documents'
bytes, every pull request runs the 12.9-minute row on every push (part 08 §6.2).

Step 3's cutover is one pull request, not a step per owning pull request (part 09, "Departures": the
lift's core is the head parser, so a step written at an intermediate head would be written against a
parser that later moves). The "Notice and transform" column therefore names the step ids and reasons
whose behavior the row brings; every one of them is written in pull request 13, the cutover, against
the head parser.

Totals the stack reaches (part 08 §1, §3): pull request 1 registers 11 live
entries, pull request 5 registers defect 21's 3 and moves defect 25's one manifest entry
(part 08 §3.7), pull request 8 moves 9 to
`proof/fixed-defects.json` (finding 56's 5 of pull request 1 and 2 of the base, and finding 75's
device half's 2), and pull request 10 moves finding 75's 4 search entries; the live register holds
293 after pull request 1, 295 after pull request 5 and 286 after pull request 8. At the exit
`proof/known-defects.json` holds 282 (the base's 282, less finding 56's two and defect 25's one,
plus defect 21's three) and `proof/fixed-defects.json` 734 (721 after pull request 5, 730 after pull
request 8). `proof/controls/` goes from 105
directories to 108 (two in pull request 1, one in pull request 5), every one named by a register.
`proof/walk-stops.json` goes from 29 held stops to 30 (pull request 1, finding 75's search error)
and to 25 (pull request 5: six removed and finding 74's stop on `fuzz-xform-20260930-0` added);
pull request 10 turns finding 75's into its control's, so 25 at the exit. `OPEN_DEFECTS` goes from
ten defects to eleven (pull request 1), to ten (pull request 8) and
to nine (pull request 10), every one a later step's. No spelling rule is added or retired: `RULES` holds
25 throughout. `proof/identity-moves.json` stays empty (part 08 §3.5). The corpus gains thirteen
targeted documents and eight fuzz documents (part 08 §4.3, §4.5). `MODEL_CONTEXT_VERSION` moves five
times, in pull requests 3, 5, 9, 10 and 12. `RUNTIME_VERSION` moves once, in 13.

The single defect 25 move is a gate effect (part 08 §3.7). In `container-nested-query`,
`child_ids.calculate` now holds a `repeat-row` read of the outer query repeat's identity. Converting
that repeat to a group, the exact old edit, strands the row identity and is refused by
`lib/doc/commitVerdicts.ts::mutationCommitVerdict` as `INVALID_REF` (`unknown-form-field`,
`if.cond.left`, `dangling-identity`). The corpus's edit planner therefore chooses another admitted
edit, whose D′ lacks this exact manifest symptom; the retained control keeps the old conversion
and still shows it. The other defect 25 entries and `OPEN_DEFECTS[25] = 5` stay live; this move
claims no repair to HQ's model iteration.

## Files, by pull request

This is the list an implementer holds a pull request's diff against. Under each pull request it
names every file the parts' Files sections and text give it, by name or by the directory that holds
it. Paths are written from the repository root. A file is named outright where the pull request
creates, deletes or moves it ("new", "deleted", "moved") and where it is a test the change turns.
What the change is for is in the stack's Contains cell and in the part.

Five kinds of file are given once, here. A source file stands for its tests in the `__tests__`
directory beside it. A pull request that moves or adds an entry edits `proof/known-defects.json`
and, for a move, `proof/fixed-defects.json`. A pull request that adds a control directory or a corpus
document edits `proof/timings.json` (both profiles where the group is in both). A `CLAUDE.md` file or
a public docs page is listed under the pull request whose change makes its sentence false, as
"Contract sentences step 3 rewrites" assigns it; the architecture docs but `deployment.md` are
pull request 14's.

And **every importer of a module a pull request deletes, moves or re-signs is re-pointed in that
pull request**, tests, fixtures, scripts, lane producers and comments that cite the module
included, because each pull request is green at its own head. Their list is a command run at the
pull request's base, not a list kept here. For a module `<dir>/<name>.ts`, with `<last>` the last
segment of `<dir>`:

```
rg -l -e '<dir>/<name>\b' -e '<dir>"' -e '"\./<name>"' -e '"(\.\./)+<last>(/<name>)?"' \
  lib app components proof scripts e2e __tests__
```

The first pattern finds absolute imports, comments and the path strings a lane producer reads
with `readFileSync`; the second a barrel import of the directory; the third and fourth relative
imports. A module a barrel re-exports is also reached through the barrel's own path, which none of
these patterns names: `lib/domain/index.ts` re-exports `./xpath` (`export * from "./xpath"`), so a
file that imports `xpathExpressionSchema` or `XPathExpression` from `@/lib/domain` reaches
`lib/domain/xpath` unseen. So beside the path search, a symbol search runs over every name the
module exports, through every barrel that re-exports it:

```
rg -l -w -e '<export 1>' -e '<export 2>' … lib app components proof scripts e2e __tests__
```

and its hits outside the module are read like the path search's. Each hit is read, since a relative
hit can name another directory of the same last segment and a symbol hit can be a same-named local.
Most hits change only an import path or a type name; the non-mechanical ones, which change what
they do, are named under their pull request.

## What every pull request of the stack does

- **It is green at its own head.** Removing one fix's entries can leave another entry holding nothing
  on its document, which `proof/checks/registers.py::reconcile` refuses, so each pull request's lane
  passes at its head, not only the stack's.
- **A stored-shape change lands in pull request 5 only.** The union, every slot that holds it, the
  catalog's retired rule fields and the form link's datum name change shape there and nowhere else, so every
  document's key moves once (part 08 §4.6), and the cutover's lift targets one shape. A pull request
  above it may widen what a profile admits (pull requests 9 and 10) but stores no new shape.
  Pull request 5 stays one pull request, large as it is: a stored shape, its only writers and
  readers, and the corpus that must speak it to keep the lane green cannot land apart, so any split
  leaves a head where the document stores what no reader reads or the corpus no longer parses. Its
  size is met instead by settling it on its own branch before the stack is cut (house rule 5).
- **A served-schema change is found by hash.** Every pull request runs
  `scripts/inspect-tool-schema-hashes.ts` at its base and head (free, no network) and records in its
  description the tools whose hash moved; only pull request 3 and the pull requests that change a
  tool on purpose show one. A tool whose hash moves is registered in `scripts/test-schema.ts` by the
  pull request that first moves it.
- **`MODEL_CONTEXT_VERSION` moves in each pull request that changes model-facing schema or prose**:
  the name, description or input schema of a tool the Solutions
  Architect is offered, the authoring guide, or what an authored text input accepts. Checklist item
  13 gives the five.
- **A validator narrowing runs the corpus emission first** (no Docker, `--jobs 2`), at base and
  head, and compares the stop, the census, `index.json`'s document ids and the `(document, code,
  location)` finding multiset (part 03 §7, part 08 §4.1). A document the narrowing refuses is
  rewritten in its producer with every entity it gains named, or, for a fuzz draw, mapped after the
  draw (part 08 §4.2); a fuzz sample is never drawn again. **Implementation correction,
  2026-10-19:** every admission point refuses on the first finding, so that multiset is empty by
  construction; the comparison is head code validating the base corpus's D and D′ (a read-only
  runner kept outside the repository), `index.json`'s census and each `edit/batch.json` diffed,
  then the head emission.
- **A new producer scenario is appended with every entity named,** and the pull request diffs every
  fixed document's `edit/batch.json` at base and head; a scenario that moves another document's batch
  becomes a targeted document instead (part 08 §2.6).
- **A manifest flip** edits its entry in `lib/commcare/surface/entries/<area>.json` and its row in
  `docs/research/2026-09-26-hq-round-trip/inventory/<area>.md` in the pull request that makes the
  construct emittable (part 08 §5.1).
- **A pull request that changes which flags a search needs** writes `gates.json`'s `effects` again
  when `test_sensitivity.py` fails, from a run where every group ran (`gh workflow run ci.yml --ref
  <branch> -f proof_fresh=true`, then `python3 -m proof.checks.sensitivity effects …`). Pull
  request 10 is the one expected to (part 08 §5).
- **No pull request adds an `/api` route.** One added while implementing takes its `lib/hostnames.ts`
  allowlist entry in the same pull request, or the proxy answers 404 in production while localhost
  serves it.
- **No pull request runs a served reader on the person's machine.** A reader run is a dispatch on
  GitHub's runners, started by the person (part 08 §6.4).

## Ordering constraints

| Constraint | Reason |
|---|---|
| This plan's own pull request beneath the stack | It writes the deferral notes into `4-platforms.md`, `5-case-writes-forms-navigation.md`, `6-import.md` and `7-rest-of-model.md` ("Written by this plan's own pull request"), so everything step 3 leaves is recorded in the step that receives it before any of step 3's code lands; and it re-points the two citations of the outline's "Carried from step 2" to the top file's "The baseline this plan was written against" ("Citations of the outline"), so no link dangles from its merge to pull request 15 |
| Issue 768's fix (#771) on `main` before the cut | The first night after the merge reads every document with every reader, and without the fix that night ends in the Android reader's runtime job (house rule 8) |
| Lane mechanics (1) is first | A control is retained from the pre-fix corpus and a fix moves entries into a register only where they were registered: finding 56's and finding 75's controls must be captured before pull requests 8 and 10 silence them, and the zoned harness must exist before any zoned class (7, 8, 10) |
| The cutover skeleton (2) sits beneath every `lib` change | The frozen fixtures must be validated against the pre-step schema and gate, the frozen printer held equal to today's `projectXPath`, and the base emitter must emit with production's emitter. Pull request 2's head is production's `lib` and `app` plus test modules, so the census's base half runs there and the normalizer, never edited after it, serves both halves (part 09 §3) |
| The projection (3) beneath the shape (5) | `authoringJsonSchema`'s pre-substitution renumbers the `__schemaN` definitions once against today's model, so pull request 5, which changes the canonical model, shows no served hash moved (part 01 §6.3, part 02 §3.6). The retired catalog rules' three tool inputs ride 3 so every served-schema change of the model lands in one pull request (the `datum` family of 5 serves today's bytes); the stored fields leave in 5, and between the two a stored value is still validated and cannot be authored |
| The wire tree (4) beneath the shape (5) | The emitter restructure is reviewed apart from the stored shape, and the count of fresh 12.9-minute lanes is the same either way. 4's settled-before-cut runs (base-and-head emission, slices) apply to it as to 5 (part 08 §6.2) |
| The shape (5) carries the session datum identity | The corpus already holds a form link reading a new case's datum (`b.session.data.case_id_new_<type>_<n>`, one in the corpus), and production holds `selected-case` reads (part 05 §6.1). Pull request 5's parser must resolve every read stored today (part 02 §9.2), so `DatumRef`, `FormLinkDatumName`, `entryDatumSupplies`, the `datum` family, the datum parse refusals and `SESSION_DATUM_NOT_SUPPLIED` over identity land there, and entry supply is the checker's. `targeted-session-reads` lands with them, so the pull request that changes datum reads carries their lane evidence; the new instance reads' documents and scenarios stay in 9 |
| Preview's session map (7) above the shape (5) | From 7 the Preview target prints `#<type>/<prop>` as the wire's casedb walk keyed on `session/data/<datum>`, so without `entrySessionData` every case read of a follow-up form would read blank in Preview from 7; the map needs only `entryDatumIds` (5), the ids over `entryDatumSupplies`'s refs, and today's menu case map, so it rides 7 with the submission's minted ids (part 07 §8) |
| The shape (5) re-points the builder's linter and carries every card a stored kind needs | `lib/commcare/validator/xpathValidator.ts` and `validator/timeOrdering.ts`, which `lib/codemirror/xpath-lint.ts` and `buildLintContext.ts::formTimeOrderings` call, leave in 5, and each pull request is green at its head with the Playwright smoke. So 5 re-points the linter to the parser and the slot checker with spans, and rewrites the `lib/codemirror/CLAUDE.md` sentences the re-point makes false; that file's other sentences change in 11. And from 5 the record dialect builds `negate` and the parser builds `choice`, so "every kind in both discriminator unions has a card" (`components/builder/CLAUDE.md`, held by `validByConstruction.test.ts`) needs `NegateCard`, `HereCard` and the display-only `ValueListCard` there. 11 does the rest of part 02 §4: the menus that offer them, chips, completion, reconciliation, the profile-derived axes |
| The kind-conversion `choice` step (5) with the `choice` leaf | From 5 a literal opposite a static select is a `choice`; an `editField` kind conversion or a `setFieldOptionsSource` replacement between 5 and 11 would otherwise leave a `choice` naming options that no longer exist, which the gate refuses. So `lib/doc/kindConversionCascade.ts`'s step lands in 5, and only the options editor's controls wait for 11 |
| The form-logic lookup extractors (9) with the reads | A form-logic table read is admitted from 9 (its `toWire` row and declaration land there), so the four extractors of `lib/doc/lookupReferences.ts` land with it, and with them the publish push and the deletion refusal that follow |
| Completion sources (9) before span chips (11) | 9's completion sources extend today's Lezer-node detection in `xpath-autocomplete.ts`, which part 02 §4.2 keeps; the chips of 9's reads need 11's span-based chips, so they ride 11 |
| The checker (6) above the shape (5) | Its rules read the typed tree and the profiles 5 lands. Its narrowings run their own base-and-head emission, so a refused corpus document is named in 6 alone |
| Preview (7) beneath the lowering (8) | Preview evaluates what the device evaluates (part 07 §1.2): the lowering is shared code only once Preview prints through `toWire` at `target: "preview"`, and `instantLowering.test.ts` (8) evaluates that print. Preview's date fixes (§2) are what make Preview's answer the instant order before 8 makes the device's (part 07 §8 item 1) |
| The zoned harness (1) beneath `CoreDateParityRuntimeTest` (7) and `CsqlCompositionRuntimeTest` (10) | Each is a zoned class (part 08 §2.1) |
| The lowering (8) beneath instances and session (9) | The day rule over results rows (part 04 §3.4) applies the day rule 8 brings to the rows 9 makes readable |
| Preview's search population (7) beneath the search-results snapshot (9) | The snapshot reads the Project's open cases without the restore scope, the population 7 decides (part 07 §3.5) |
| Instances and session (9) and the lowering (8) beneath CSQL (10) | Every CSQL runtime segment takes the on-device lowering and may hold the instance reads 9 admits (part 06 decision 19) |
| The chosen case in record slots (10) above the shape (5) and Preview (7) | It reads `DatumRef` and `lib/domain/entryDatumSupplies.ts` (5) and Preview's `entrySessionData`, which serves the Search screen from 7, so nothing a later step owns is needed; it rides 10 because a search's chosen-case read is a CSQL runtime segment, whose positions, emission and fold land there (part 06 §3.9) |
| The builder (11) above 5, 9 and 10 | 11 covers the reads that exist from 5, and the chips of 9's reads from parse spans; the completion sources of 9's reads ride 9, the cards for new stored kinds 5, and part 06's cards 10 (part 02 §9) |
| Authoring (12) above every tool and prose change | It is the last tool change, so the billed check asked at its head is the stack's one ask, and the guide teaches only what the gate admits |
| The cutover (13) above authoring (12) | The lift's parser is the head parser (part 09 decision 2); nothing above 13 changes a parser, a schema or a gate |
| Contracts and public docs (14) beneath the removal (15) | The maintenance image is built from 14's head and the Job is managed from a checkout of it, because the tool and its allowlist arm exist only there; the window runs from its image |
| The removal (15) differs from 14 only in tooling and the plan's own documents | It also removes this plan, its part directory and the runbook, and writes the plans `README.md`'s step 3 rows, as step 2's #767 did. The gate that migrated must be the gate the deploy's runtime probe runs (`lib/db/runtimeDatabaseProbe.ts::runCanonicalRuntimeDatabaseProbe`). 15 touches nothing under `lib/domain`, `lib/commcare`, `lib/doc`, `lib/case-store`, `lib/preview` or `app`, and under `lib/db` only the two exports of `designSessions.ts` and the writer's entry in `__tests__/runHolderWriteGuard.test.ts` |
| Every migration of the stack is additive | The execute run applies the whole pending ledger before the merge (`lib/case-store/migrate.ts::runCaseStoreMigrationsWithReport`), with the old revision still serving; `writer.postgres.test.ts` starts from `main`'s migration prefix and finds a migration that is not (part 09 §1). After the first rehearsal a fix to a migration is a new migration file, never an edit |

## House rules for the stack

1. **One chain on the latest `main`.** The stack is one `gh stack` chain cut from the latest
   `origin/main` after this plan's pull request merges, and rebased on it before the first pull
   request opens. Every pull request is undrafted.
2. **`/code-review medium` runs on each pull request**, and its findings are fixed there. Where the
   review's agents sit idle for about a minute with no notification, their findings are collected by
   hand.
3. **The stack merges together** with `gh stack merge <top> --squash`: one push, one deploy. Never
   pull request by pull request, and never through the merge API. Each pull request stays one squash
   commit in `main`'s history.
4. **Fix-ups go to the pull request they belong in, and a session's pushes are batched into one.** A
   push restacks every pull request above it, each a fresh lane: a push to pull request 1 restacks all
   fifteen, 186 shard-jobs (three runs of 6 shards and twelve of 14) against the account's twenty
   concurrent runners, about two hours of wall time; a push to pull request 5 starts eleven 12.9-minute
   runs, 154 shard-jobs, about an hour and a half (part 08 §6.1, §6.2, arithmetic on its rows). Pull
   request 1's fix-ups are worth batching most, since it sits under every other.
5. **Pull requests 4 and 5 are settled before the stack is cut.** Each one's base-and-head emission
   (no Docker, `--jobs 2`), its slices without readers on GitHub's runners (`gh workflow run ci.yml
   --ref <branch>`) and, for 5, R5 (part 08 §6.4; `gh workflow run proof-audit.yml --ref <branch> -f
   nightly=false -f mechanics=fuzz-expression-20260930-0,…,fuzz-expression-20260930-7,targeted-expression-renames,targeted-expression-property-renames,targeted-session-reads,targeted-list-first-places,targeted-form-link-session-reads,targeted-nested-menu-session-reads,targeted-tile-session-reads,fuzz-suite-20260930-5,fuzz-suite-20260930-8,fuzz-xform-20260930-0,fuzz-xform-20260930-3,fuzz-xform-20260930-4,fuzz-xform-20260930-9`)
   run on its own branch first, because a change to either after the cut restacks ten or more fresh
   lanes.
6. **No heavy run on this machine.** The person works on the laptop the agents run on. The no-Docker
   host emission with `--jobs 2` and pure Vitest files are the only runs that belong there without
   asking; every lane slice, the native families, a fresh lane and every reader run go to GitHub's
   runners (part 08 §6.3).
7. **The billed schema check is asked once, at pull request 12's head**, the stack's last tool
   change, naming every registered tool whose hash moved across the stack (base `main`, head 12), as
   checklist item 14 gives it. On a yes the implementer runs `npm run test:schema -- <name>` once per
   name, never the bare command. A schema the provider refuses is fixed in the pull request that owns
   it, and the question is asked again for that schema alone.
8. **Issue 768's fix is #771**, stacked on the plan's pull request and merged with it, so it is on
   `main` before the stack is cut. The Android reader's runtime job has failed
   `main`'s own nightly since 2026-10-14, and planning's first with-readers dispatches (runs
   39058712962, 39058666752, 39058667355) died in it; the no-reader runs held (operands 39061011794
   but for the manifest classes pull request 1 registers; spellings 39061476968 clean). Planning
   wrote the fix (proof-side only, commit `ee852ae9`; part 08 §6.4) and proved it: the second dispatches, with it
   beneath each scratch change (R1 run 39064331941, R2 run 39064333759, R3 and R4 run 39064336028),
   pass that job, and R2's held with every reader. R3 and R4's showed no reader's own end past the
   six opened forms, and two things pull request 5 carries: the fix to the Formplayer walk's model
   of an answer a question already holds (`proof/formplayer/walk.py`), and defect 21's two live
   entries on `targeted-list-first-places`, since the register refuses an entry on the fuzz
   document that showed them (part 08 §3.3). R1's first shard held, with every served reader
   reproducing finding 56's literal case-list half; its second shard lost its hosted runner, and
   R1 run 39078199859 read its three documents with every reader: `targeted-datetime-equality` as
   the plan writes it shows finding 75's two device-half entries and no finding 74 stop, and HQ's
   500 for "Opened visits", which pull request 1 holds in four entries and a walk stop (part 06
   §3.13). What each run settles is part 08 §6.4's table, and what a reader shows past an
   opened form is added to `walk-stops.json` (a reader's own end), registered under the known defect
   it shows, fixed in the harness where it is the harness's own model, or made a finding with a
   targeted document, in pull request 1 or 5 as its owner. No reader run is required before the merge; the
   fix is required, because the first night after the merge reads every document with every reader
   (house rule 11) and without it ends in that job.
9. **The cutover runs before the merge** (part 09 §14). The maintenance image is built by hand
   (`docker build --platform=linux/amd64 --target=maintenance`) from pull request 14's head, and both
   Jobs (`commcare-nova-historical-repair`, `commcare-nova-case-type-schema-retirement`) are made from
   that image and managed from a checkout of that commit. `main` is frozen from the image build to the
   merge; a rebase after the image is built rebuilds it and repeats the dry run. The runbook records
   pull request 14's head commit and the image digest. The window's order, its checks and its
   rollback are part 09 §14's.
10. **After the merge, the deploy is watched on the merge commit with `gh`**: the commit's check-runs
    are listed first and the watch is built from the app name observed there. Pull request CI being
    green is not a deploy, and this deploy runs the runtime probe, because the stack changes
    `lib/domain` and the validator. The build's `verify` step fails while the NEG is detached, as step
    2's did: wait for `deploy` to report the revision ready, then converge the case store and retry the
    build (part 09 §14, steps 9 to 11).
11. **The first nightly after the merge is the whole lane with every reader.** The merge moves every
    key, so the next scheduled night (Tuesday to Saturday, 03:23 UTC) runs about 13.5 hours on up to
    fourteen of the twenty runners (part 08 §6.4); a merge from Saturday evening to Monday is read by
    Tuesday's. A night that fails opens an issue naming each failing group with the command that
    reruns it alone (`python3 -m proof.lane.report`); the implementer fixes from that list and reruns
    only the documents on it, by name. The `readers` timings follow that night in an ordinary pull
    request.
12. **The `../nova-plugin` check rides pull request 12** ("The nova-plugin check", below).

Removing the tooling at the top of the same stack, not in a follow-up pull request, is step 2's
stated choice kept: a later removal would be a second deploy that exists only because of the split,
and the run, its post-migration scan and its drift scan all precede the merge.

## The model-addition checklist

Each addition is a complete feature when its pull request lands, and its author ticks every place
below or writes "none" with the reason. Step 3's additions, by part:

- **Arms** (part 01 §1.4): `negate`, `call` (the 60 JavaRosa functions no arm means, plus
  `normalize-space`), `cases` (`casedb` and `search-results`), `table-rows`, `locations`,
  `selected-cases`, `project`, `itext`, `choice-name`, `here`, `value-list` (held for import), and
  `arith`'s `quotient`. Widened: `table-lookup.where`, `if.cond`, `switch` and `in` admitting
  `choice`, `count.via`, `format-date.pattern`, `within-distance` (runtime distance and unit, nine
  units), `match.property`, `multi-select-contains` (calculated subject, runtime values),
  `matches-pattern.pattern`, the logical arms over `Expression`, `date-coerce` and `datetime-coerce`
  (numeric operands).
- **Leaves** (part 01 §1.3): `choice`, `case-type`, `case-row-type`, `case-index`, `session-datum`
  (`DatumRef`, five kinds), `repeat-row`, `location-level`, `location-field`; widened `field`
  (`spelling`, `climb`, `filters`), `session-user` and `session-user-property` (`source`),
  `session-context` (seven fields stored, four authored).
- **Supporting shapes**: `FormLinkDatumName`, `ItextUnit`, `LocationRowField`, `FieldStepFilter`
  (part 01 §1.2).
- **Profiles** (part 01 §4): one per stored slot in `lib/domain/expression/profiles.ts`; the catalog
  profile does not exist, because the catalog's rules retire.
- **Instance reads** (part 05): `casedb` queries typed, data tables in form logic and form links,
  flat `locations`, a batch form's selected cases, search results in an inline-search module's forms,
  session datums; in record slots, the case chosen in a menu the entry passes through (10).
- **CSQL constructs** (part 06 §3, §6): `closed_on`, `record-type()`, epoch coercions, runtime
  `selected*` values, nine units and runtime distance, runtime choice, the closed instant range, the
  flag-free ancestor spelling, the chosen case and its properties, `value-list`.
- **Validation codes**: `SELECT_DEFAULT_NOT_AN_OPTION` (5); `XPATH_INCOMPATIBLE_VALUES` (reasons
  `date-datetime`, `datetime-ordering`, `boolean`), `XPATH_MANY_VALUES`,
  `HIDDEN_VALUE_WRITER_TYPE_MISMATCH`, `SELECT_PRELOAD_WRITE_NOT_AN_OPTION` and the reasons
  `datetime-literal-precision`, `format-date-pattern-not-fixed`, `number-of-date` (6); the reason
  `day-only-on-device` (8); `SEARCH_RESULTS_NOT_SUPPLIED`, `SEARCH_RESULTS_READ_INVALID`,
  `LOCATIONS_READ_NEEDS_ORGANIZATION` (9); the oracle's `XFORM_UNREAD_INSTANCE`,
  `SUITE_UNREAD_INSTANCE` (4); part 06's CSQL reasons and `CASE_LIST_CLOSED_ON_NOT_ON_DEVICE`
  (`closed-on-not-on-device`) (10). Retired: the fifteen syntax codes, `CONNECT_EMPTY_XPATH`,
  `CASE_PROPERTY_XPATH_INCOMPATIBLE` (5). Each new code and reason lands with its rows in the
  typed-total tables of place 2, in the pull request named beside it.

The places:

1. **Schema** (`lib/domain/expression/types.ts`): the arm or leaf in `termSchema` or
   `expressionSchema`, with its hand-declared TypeScript arm and the drift guard; a slot holding it
   takes `formLogicSchema`, `conditionSchema` or `valueSchema` by family (part 01 §6.2). A stored
   shape lands in pull request 5 only.
2. **Checker** (`lib/domain/expression/typeChecker.ts`, `ruleSets.ts`, `context.ts`): its type on
   both axes (part 01 §3), each rule set's verdict over it, its admission in every profile that takes
   it (`profiles.ts`), and the copy of each refusal in `lib/doc/userFacingErrors.ts` and the validator
   `message`, written person to person: what was written, why CommCare cannot run it, what to write
   instead. A new `ValidationErrorCode` takes its row in each typed-total table in its own pull
   request, or that pull request does not compile: `lib/commcare/validator/errors.ts` (the code),
   `gate.ts::VALIDITY_CLASS_BY_CODE` (its class), `lib/doc/userFacingErrors.ts` (its sentence, over
   every non-oracle code; a code with several reasons picks its sentence by `details.reason`), and a
   new checker code its line in `components/builder/shared/checkErrorPresentation.ts::PRESENT_BY_CODE`.
   An oracle code (class `oracle`) takes the first two only.
3. **Both grammars and both printers**: the form-XPath parser (`lib/commcare/xpath/expressionAst.ts`,
   through the Lezer grammar `lib/commcare/xpath/grammar.lezer.grammar`, never a regex) and the
   form-XPath printer (`lib/domain/expression/print.ts`); the record dialect's parser and printer
   (`lib/agent/authoring/queryExpressions.ts`, `printQueryExpression.ts`) where the arm is in a record
   profile. The round-trip law `parse(print(e)) = e` holds per grammar, in the fast-check suites (part
   02 §2.4). A shape the grammar cannot read is added to the grammar and the parser rebuilt.
4. **Wire** (`lib/commcare/xpath/wire/fromExpression.ts`): its `toWire` row for every target the
   profile names, its instance declaration in `instances.ts` (part 05 §2.1's table), and, where the
   serializer must spell it so HQ or Vellum keeps it, the mode's rule in `serialize.ts`; the
   oracles, the surface manifest entry, and `lib/commcare/CLAUDE.md`. A CSQL construct takes its
   position rule in `lib/commcare/csql/positions.ts` and its `CsqlNode` in `emitter.ts`.
5. **Preview** (`lib/preview`): the instance it supplies for the read (`engine/xpathInstances.ts`),
   any function it evaluates (`xpath/functions.ts`), its dependency edge if it reads a field
   (`walk.ts::fieldReads`); `lib/preview/CLAUDE.md`.
6. **Postgres** (`lib/case-store/sql`): its compile under the right reading, two-valued over a
   missing value (part 07 §5.5), with values as bound parameters; or "none" where no Postgres target
   takes it.
7. **Index and renames** (`lib/doc/referenceIndex.ts`, `lib/domain/referenceSlots.ts`): the edge
   under an existing prefix, what a rename, move or removal does to it (part 01 §5), and the
   incremental ≡ rebuild oracle (`lib/doc/__tests__/referenceIndex.fuzz.test.ts`); lookup reads join
   `lib/doc/lookupReferences.ts`'s extractors, never the index.
8. **Builder cards and completion**: a card for every kind in both discriminator unions
   (`components/builder/shared/cards/`, registered in `shared/editorSchemas.ts`; a held kind gets a
   display-only card), offered only where `editorAxesForSlot` admits it; autocomplete and chips
   (`lib/codemirror/xpath-autocomplete.ts`, `xpath-chips.ts`) for a form-logic read; state in a pure
   model with its own test, Playwright for the interaction; `components/builder/CLAUDE.md`,
   `lib/codemirror/CLAUDE.md`.
9. **SA and MCP**: the decoder and encoder (`lib/agent/authoring/input.ts`, `output.ts`), the family
   map (`schema.ts`), the authoring guide where it teaches the read (`reference.ts`), and
   `operationSemantics.ts` where it reads records. MCP serves the same schemas
   (`lib/mcp/adapters`); `lib/mcp/__tests__/servedSchemas.test.ts` holds what a client receives.
10. **Public docs**: the `content/docs` page for the task a reader does and `content/docs/mcp/tools.mdx`
    for the callable reference, in Nova's voice.
11. **Proof**: the native family that holds its meaning (an existing family's new scenario or a new
    family), a targeted document with explicit identities and hand-fixed values where no corpus
    source draws it, the `expression` generator's shape where the gate admits it, the manifest flip
    and, where a REFUSED class names it, its value-class reader (part 08 §5.2).
12. **Existing documents**: a class of a cutover step (part 09 §5), its notice reason where a person
    or worker sees a change (§12), its blocker where the lift can refuse (§11), and its fixture under
    `scripts/lib/hqRoundTripExpressions/__tests__/fixtures/pre-step/`; or "none: production holds no
    stored value of it", as the read-only production scans run during planning show (part 09).
13. **`MODEL_CONTEXT_VERSION`** (`lib/models.ts`) moves in each pull request that changes the name,
    description or input schema of a tool the Solutions Architect is offered, the authoring guide, or
    what an authored text input accepts (step 2's precedent: a refinement that narrows an input bumps
    it). Each bump is a distinct literal, so every head states its own contract; the stack deploys
    once, so production takes pull request 12's. The five:

    | PR | Change | Value |
    |---|---|---|
    | 3 | the served-schema renumbering and the retired catalog rules' three tool inputs | `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions"` (from `"v3-hq-round-trip-emission.14"`) |
    | 5 | the form-XPath parser refuses authored text today's decoder keeps as text (`case-query-untyped`, `unknown-datum`, `instance('groups')`, a wildcard step), and admits relative paths | `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions.5"` |
    | 9 | the guide's data table, places, several-case and search-first sentences (part 02 §3.1 items 3 and 6) | `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions.9"` |
    | 10 | `record-type()`, the chosen-case sentence and part 06 §7's clauses; what an authored record text accepts (`chosen-case`, `chosen-case-property`); any CSQL tool description the hash run shows moved | `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions.10"` |
    | 12 | the guide's relative-path, case-query, operation, `here()`, fields and forms sentences | `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions.12"` |

    Pull requests 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 11, 13, 14 and 15 leave it: no tool's name, description or

## Contract sentences step 3 rewrites

Each "Today" was read in today's file at `main` `5cf6e327` and is quoted whole, or, for a long
passage, by its opening and its closing words in two quotes; where the source wraps a sentence over
lines, the quote joins them with one space (the `lib/commcare` and `docs/architecture` quotes were
held exact by a script, 124 of 124). A section is named by its heading or its opening words, never by
a line. "Kept; adds" means the quoted sentence stays and the new text follows it. A replacement in
quotation marks is the text as written, and it names no plan step, pull request, part, defect or
finding, because the plan leaves the repository in pull request 15 and a contract file says what is
so; a note outside quotation marks is for the editor. The proof lane's documents and the research's
findings are the exception: they name steps, defects and findings today and keep doing so.

"PR" is the pull request whose change makes the sentence false, and a contract sentence moves with
the behavior that makes it false, so every `CLAUDE.md` file is true at every head; where one sentence
goes false in two pull requests it has a row per pull request. A row with no single owner is 14's.
Three placements follow from "Ordering constraints": a row about session datum identity
(`DatumRef`, `entryDatumSupplies`, a form link's datum name, `SESSION_DATUM_NOT_SUPPLIED` over
identity, `rules/sessionData.ts` leaving) and every `lib/codemirror/CLAUDE.md` sentence the linter's
re-point makes false land in 5; a row about the new reads part 05 makes authorable (data tables and
places in form logic, selected cases, search results, `jr:itext`, `jr:choice-name`, Preview's results
snapshot) stays 9; and Preview's session map (`entrySessionData`) lands in 7, with every sentence
that it makes false at pull request 7's head: the entry's datums in `session/data`, the ids the entry
mints and the submission creates with (`case-id-taken`), and the entry key each App Test session and
each authoring evaluation mints.

Public docs move with behavior too: each public docs row below lands in the pull request whose
behavior it describes. Pull request 14 takes the architecture docs (`docs/architecture/complex-apps.md`,
`contracts.md` and `multilingual-localization.md`, whose "PR" cells name the pull request whose
behavior each row describes) and every row no single pull request owns, as step 2's contracts pull
request did. `docs/architecture/deployment.md` names tooling, so its rows ride the pull requests that
add or remove it (5, 13 and 15).

#### lib/domain/predicate/CLAUDE.md

The file is deleted with the package in PR 5. `lib/domain/expression/CLAUDE.md` (new, PR 5; part 01 §6.1, "rewritten for the model (outline, 'Contracts')") replaces it. Its sections, in today's order:

| Section | Today | From step 3 | PR |
|---|---|---|---|
| title and opening | "# lib/domain/predicate — Predicate + ValueExpression AST" … "The single package owning Nova's two structurally-related AST families: `Predicate` (boolean) and `ValueExpression` (typed values)." | "# lib/domain/expression: the Expression union" … "The one stored union every expression slot holds, form logic and record slots alike. Being a condition is a type, not a family: a slot that wants a condition requires `boolean`. The Zod schemas in `types.ts` are the shape reference; this file holds only what the schemas can't say." | 5 |
| "Why two families share one package" | "Predicate operators carry `ValueExpression` operands, and `ValueExpression`'s `if` / `switch` / `count` carry `Predicate` clauses." … "One package lets both unions reach each other." | Section replaced by "One recursive union": "`expressionSchema` is one `z.discriminatedUnion("kind", …)` whose recursive slots are `z.lazy(() => expressionSchema)`, with hand-declared TypeScript arms and a drift guard (Zod issues #4264, #5035). `formLogicSchema`, `conditionSchema` and `valueSchema` are three distinct `z.lazy` wrappers over that union: they parse one language, and the family decides only the authoring grammar and the served description. `ConditionArm`, `ValueArm` and `InstanceArm` are named subsets for narrowing, not stored distinctions." | 5 |
| "Non-obvious arm facts" | "`in.values` and `multi-select-contains` are literal-only because the wire targets expect static lists." | "`in.values` and `switch` cases hold literals or `choice` leaves. `multi-select-contains` values admit computed values where the slot's profile admits them (in a slot a device evaluates, only a single-choice value)." | 5 |
| same | "`Term` has NO value-expression arm — cross-family composition lives one level up (`ValueExpression.term` lifts any Term; Predicate operators take `ValueExpression` directly)." | "Every leaf enters through the `term` arm, in every slot, and `Term` has no expression arm. Row leaves (`prop`, `case-index`, `case-row-type`, `table-column`, `location-field`) read the row of the nearest enclosing row-scoped construct; a row leaf with no row in scope is the checker's `row-scope`." | 5 |
| same, custom worker bullet | "Custom worker information is `session-user-property { userPropertyUuid }`, stored by stable identity and resolved to its current slug only at Preview, SQL, or CommCare emission." | kept; adds "`source: "worker-record"` reads the worker's own case (a form's `#user/`); no `source` reads session user data." | 5 |
| "Blank semantics — locked invariant", "`matches-pattern`", "Exact calendar-day search", "Relation-read target contracts", "JSON Schema generator", "Barrel" | (as today) | carried over unchanged, with `TypeContext` read as the profile-built `ExpressionContext` (its `patternMatching` comes from the slot's profile); the first heading is written "Blank semantics: a locked invariant", and the `matches-pattern` heading "`matches-pattern`: one leaf, one runtime". The barrel re-exports from `expression/` only: no shim at the old paths. | 5 |
| "Type checker contract" | "`checkPredicate` / `checkExpression` validate a constructed AST against the case-type schema + search-input declarations, with per-node error paths." | "`checkExpression` checks a tree under one of two rule sets chosen by the slot's profile, with per-node error paths. Record slots keep the rules below exactly, and keep condition and value positions as a rule (`record-position`). Form logic takes Core's coercions and refuses only what Core refuses or what can never be right: `incompatible-values`, `many-values` (a repeat's answer read from outside the repeat, or a several-case selection, used as one value), `time-ordering`." | 5 |
| same | "`checkComparison` and `checkBetween` give it a code of its own, `time-ordering`, ahead of the ordered-types test;" | "`time-ordering` is one rule for both rule sets, ahead of the ordered-types test;" | 5 |
| "Named tool-schema definitions" | "The AST family schemas carry registered ids (`z.globalRegistry.add`, end of `types.ts`), so every `z.toJSONSchema` emission" … "A new reused-or-recursive family member should get an id here too." | In predicate/CLAUDE.md at PR 3: "The authoring projection (`lib/agent/authoring/schema.ts::authoringJsonSchema`) substitutes each family position with its family's string schema before Zod walks a tool's input, so the served bytes depend only on the authored surface and no change to this package moves them. The registry ids still name the leaves for any other `z.toJSONSchema` caller. Register via `globalRegistry.add` (attaches in place), never `.meta()`." In expression/CLAUDE.md at PR 5 it adds: "The ids `Predicate` and `ValueExpression` name `conditionSchema` and `valueSchema`, so those served definition names and family descriptions stay; the form-logic family is unregistered." | 3, 5 |
| "Two simplification layers" | "recursing through nested predicate slots including those under a ValueExpression operand (`count.where`, `if.cond`)." | "recursing through every nested condition position (`count.where`, `if.cond`, every `where`)." | 5 |
| "Wire-emission boundary" | "Three targets consume this one AST, all from outside the package: the on-device XPath dialect, the CSQL dialect, and Postgres SQL via `lib/case-store/sql`." | "Every target consumes this one union from outside the package: `lib/commcare/xpath/wire`'s `toWire` (XForm, Vellum shadow, suite, HQ JSON and Preview modes), CSQL, and Postgres via `lib/case-store/sql`." | 5 |
| same | "Non-grammar CSQL value expressions inline as on-device XPath fragments inside a wrapper `concat(...)` — the canonical CCHQ pattern per `commcare-hq/docs/case_search_query_language.rst`." | "CSQL text is a `CsqlNode` tree printed by `lib/commcare/csql/print.ts`; a value the grammar cannot hold is a runtime segment of device XPath, which is how `today()` reaches HQ's search as the worker's day." | 10 |
| "The semantic authority" (expression/CLAUDE.md as pull request 5 wrote it; **implementation correction, 2026-10-19:** rows added) | "Form positions retain Core coercion and the existing time-ordering and literal numeric refusals. Record positions retain the typed condition/value grammar, scalar compatibility, relationship resolution, assignment representation rules, and slot-specific availability." | "Form positions keep Core's coercions and refuse only comparisons Core would answer wrongly, one finding per comparison: a time ordered before or after (`time-ordering`); a date against a date and time with no `date()` on either side, an ordering of a date and time against a known value that is none, and `true()`/`false()` against a known non-yes/no value (`incompatible-values`, reasons `date-datetime`, `datetime-ordering` and `boolean`); then a provably non-numeric literal in an ordering. A string literal opposite a typed operand takes the type its ISO date, date-and-time or `HH:MM[:SS]` shape spells. Record positions keep the typed condition/value grammar, scalar compatibility (a date/datetime pair is `incompatible-values` reason `date-datetime`), relationship resolution, assignment representation rules, and slot-specific availability." Then a new paragraph: the structural many in both rule sets (`many-values`, reasons `repeat` and `selected-cases`, node-set and pass-through positions from `expressionFunctions.ts`), `datetime-literal-precision`, `format-date-pattern-not-fixed` where the profile targets the case store, `here()` only where the profile's `here` fact holds, `choice-name` unavailable everywhere (`choice-name-dynamic` over choices not inline), the Hidden Value leaf reading inference's data type, held class and lost clock (`_any` with its held class when untyped), and `expected-value` reason `clock-lost` for a clock-lost value assigned to a date-and-time destination | 6 |
| same | "Later inference or target-admission work must not silently change this shape cutover's retained scalar rules." | deleted: the rule sets and inference are stated as they are | 6 |

The two modes differ only in two resolvers and how issues are delivered. The Builder's `parseFormExpressionForSlot(doc, slot, text, lookup)` resolves exact paths and returns issues with spans for the linter. The shared agent-authoring boundary's `parseAuthoredFormExpression(scope, text)` adds the `#form/` short-name resolver and the `#case/` alias, and throws one `AuthoringInputError` listing every issue. Both resolve relative paths from the slot's context node and bind every read to an identity before anything is stored: a field by UUID with the author's spelling kept, a case property as `(caseType, property)`, a custom `#user/<slug>` by worker-property UUID (external and built-in names stay name-backed `session-user`), a session value by what supplies it, a data table, column or place by UUID, a literal opposite a static select as a `choice`. Text that does not parse never enters storage. A re-parse of an edited slot takes the slot's stored tree as its binding hint, so a case query bound by the catalog keeps its type while that type declares the property. `parseProseReference` resolves a prose `{{name}}` through the same resolvers. `projectFormExpression` prints a tree in canonical form through current names, so a rename changes friendly `#form/first_name` text without rewriting the stored expression. `resolveCloseFieldRef` is the builder's close-condition picker twin (shown field id → target UUID). SA/MCP and build-executor text inputs bind through the shared authoring boundary before they reach canonical mutation tools." | 5 |

| `referenceIndex.ts` bullet | "Edges key on identity (target UUID / custom-worker-property UUID / `(caseType, property)` / case-type name) and record (carrier UUID, registry slot id), never character spans: a consumer that needs structure walks the named slot's typed AST/template parts." | "Edges are extracted by one walker over the `Expression` union and key on identity (target UUID / custom-worker-property UUID / `(caseType, property)` / case-type name) and record (carrier UUID, registry slot id), never character spans. | new section after "Authored case-operation emission" | (none) | "### Dates and times compare as moments on a device. A comparison of two dates and times (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `between`, and `=`, `!=`, `in` or `switch` between two date-and-time operands) is lowered inline, per operand, to zone-free integer microseconds (`xpath/wire/instant.ts::instantMicros`) wherever a device evaluates it: XForm binds, setvalues, outputs, itemset filters and repeat counts; suite datum filters, details, display conditions and search prompts; form-link conditions and datums; case operation conditions; and the runtime segments of `_xpath_query`. Each operand's instant text follows what Core holds: date-and-time text as itself; a typed instant as `format-date(coalesce(x, ''), '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%3%Z')`, so Core's `format-date` does not round it; a coerced date as its UTC midnight; and a literal folded to its integer at emission. `now()` is not hoisted, because a generated node would read the clock at another moment. An equality whose other side is not a date and time, such as the blank test `x = ''`, stays bare. `datetime-coerce` never emits `date()` on a device, because Core's `date()` of a typed `Date` rounds it to the device's day. On a device `date_opened` and `last_modified` from `casedb` are days (`CaseChildElement` holds them as `DateData`): ordered against a day-aligned operand they compare by day with unchanged bytes, and the checker refuses them ordered against any other date and time, or under `=` / `!=` against any date and time, day-aligned included. `InstantOrderingRuntimeTest` holds the lowering in every zone of the `expression` native family." | 8 |

#### lib/commcare/xpath/CLAUDE.md

| Section | Today | From step 3 | PR |

| intro | "CommCare's XPath dialect: the Lezer grammar + generated parser, the carrier capability contract, and the narrow production lowerer that turns explicitly proven Nova extensions into JavaRosa-safe XPath." | "CommCare's XPath dialect: the Lezer grammar and generated parser, the form-XPath parser that turns text into the stored `Expression`, and the wire tree (`wire/`) that every XPath byte Nova emits is built as and printed from by one serializer." | 5 |
| Layout, new bullet | (none) | "- `wire/`: `tree.ts` (`WireExpr`: string, number, comparison, arithmetic, n-ary `and` / `or`, negation, call, path, and `expansion`, which marks a case, worker or Search read), `builders.ts` (the `w.*` constructors every generated site uses), `fromExpression.ts` (`toWire(expression, ctx)`, the one emitter from a stored expression, for the targets `xform`, `suite`, `csql-runtime` and `hq-json`), `instances.ts` (each root's instance id and source, per target), and `serialize.ts` (`serializeWire(expr, mode)`). The tree is post-identity: every name in it is a current wire name, so a rename never touches a tree. It has no grouping node; parentheses are the serializer's." | 4 |
| same | (none) | adds to that bullet: "`formReads.ts` (field and form-case reads, from the form's emitted-path map)". The target list gains `preview` at PR 7. `instant.ts` (the date-and-time lowering) joins at PR 8. | 5, 7, 8 |
| "`wireSource.ts`" bullet | "- `wireSource.ts` — projects stored XPath for form emission. Both authored field-reference spellings enter the same form-path resolver, so query-repeat identities address rows rather than their derived wrapper. | "`expressionAst.ts`" bullet | "- `expressionAst.ts` — the human-editor parser half of the stored-expression pair: source text → `XPathExpression` (the typed AST in `lib/domain/xpath`, whose printer + walks live domain-side because the field/form schemas store the shape). Total over any input." … "Printing resolves an identity leaf through the property's CURRENT slug, so a rename rewrites no AST." | "- `expressionAst.ts`: `parseFormExpression(source, scope)`, text → the stored `Expression` (`lib/domain/expression`) with its spans, or closed parse issues with person-to-person messages. Total over any input. The slot's profile (`lib/domain/expression/profiles.ts`) gives the admitted arms, the scope and the context node. Every reference leaf holds identity and keeps the author's path spelling (`#form/`, absolute `/data/`, relative `../`, `current()/`). The tree stores no source trivia and prints in one canonical spelling (`lib/domain/expression/print.ts`). One exact, unique `#user/<slug>` match becomes `session-user-property { userPropertyUuid }`; built-in, external, missing or ambiguous names stay the name-backed `session-user`. Printing resolves every identity leaf through its current name, so a rename rewrites no tree." | 5 |
| "`carriers.ts`" bullet | "- `carriers.ts` — the canonical walk of every persisted `XPathExpression`, paired with its closed execution profile. Field expressions are Preview-form-owned, form-link expressions are Preview-session-owned, Connect expressions are wire-form-only, and case-property catalog constraints are wire-catalog-only. Scanners and validators consume this inventory instead of maintaining their own slot lists." | "- `carriers.ts`: the walk of every stored form-logic slot, yielding each slot's tree with its profile. This is the form HQ's form builder writes on save. In every HQ-bound mode, a literal holding `instance(` and a quote prints in pieces, so HQ's instance regex finds nothing in it. The serializer returns the instances its text reads; that set is every declaration Nova writes. `toWire` and `serializeWire` are total over what the commit gate admits; their only throws are invariants the gate decided first (an untyped comparison, a dot step in an interpolated slot, two sources for one instance id, the `#case` root outside a form filter)." | 4 |

| new section "The instant lowering" | (none) | "`instant.ts::instantMicros` builds, for an operand printed `T`, the zone-free parse of `T` into integer microseconds (days from the civil date times 86,400, plus the clock, minus the written offset, plus the rounded fraction). | "Expressions and prose store identity; text is a projection" | "Every XPath-bearing slot holds the canonical `XPathExpression` AST (`lib/domain/xpath/ast.ts`): verbatim source in `text` runs, and reference leaves that carry identity" … "so a dangling reference can never sit in a document as UUID-shaped authored XPath." | "Every expression slot, form logic and record slots alike, holds one typed tree, the `Expression` union (`lib/domain/expression`); form logic stores no text. A relative path is derived again from identity at every print, so a move that changes a field's depth changes only the print, and a move whose relative read could no longer be printed is refused. Editing an option's value reprints every comparison that names it, and an option an expression names cannot be removed. | "First-class date type in the XPath evaluator" | "XPath values include a `XPathDate` alongside string/number/boolean. `today()` returns a date (days-since-epoch internally, ISO string on coercion), so `today() + 1` yields tomorrow, not `NaN`. CommCare's arithmetic converts dates to day numbers; compatible typed emitters add the required wire coercion themselves." | "XPath values include a `XPathDate` alongside string/number/boolean. It holds its local calendar date (`calendarDays`) and, for a date and time, its instant. `today()` returns a date, so `today() + 1` yields tomorrow, not `NaN`. CommCare's arithmetic converts dates to day numbers, and Preview's day number is Core's: `xpath/coreDays.ts::coreDaysSinceEpoch` and `coreDateAdd` compute it from the browser's zone history as `DateUtils.daysSinceEpoch` does (the device-zone midnight of 1 January 1970, fixed 24-hour days, half-day rounding). That is a day off the calendar in `Pacific/Apia`, `Pacific/Enderbury`, `Pacific/Fakaofo`, `Pacific/Kiritimati` and `Pacific/Kwajalein`, as it is on a device. `toNumber`, `toDouble` and `date(<number>)` use it, and `toISOString` keeps the calendar date. `date()` rounds a date and time to its local date whether it arrives directly or through a node (`coerce.ts::toDateRounding`, Core's `FunctionUtils.toDate`). `format-date` and `format-date-for-calendar` keep a direct date's clock and round a node's (`expandDateSafe`), so `format-date(/data/dt, '%H')` is `00`, as on a device. A datetime answer is read by the offset its canonical storage text carries, never re-resolved from wall time. Text entering a typed node through a setvalue is cast as Core casts it, a repeated wall-clock hour resolving to its later occurrence as Java resolves it, and the engine stores the held instant's canonical text. Compatible typed emitters add the required wire coercion themselves." | 7 |
| "Two `Child` / `Descendant` node types in the grammar" | "The evaluator and dependency extractor use `many('Child')` plus a `Set` / `.has()` check to catch both." | "The evaluator uses `many('Child')` plus a `Set` / `.has()` check to catch both." | 7 |
| "Form engine lifecycle rules" | "**Typed answers are checked before authored validation.**" (bullet) | Kept; adds a bullet after it: "**A setvalue casts as Core casts, and refuses as Core refuses.** Every value Preview writes into a typed node outside a calculate (a case preload, a starting value, a repeat insert's defaults) goes through `engine/answerCast.ts::castAnswerText(bindType, text)`, the port of `SetValueAction`'s cast. Its `bindType` comes from the XForm emitter's own bind-type function, so a Hidden Value binds by its writer destination. Blank text never casts. A cast that succeeds stores the held value's canonical text. One that fails at activation makes the entry `refused`: a terminal state beside the missing-selected-record state, with no controls and a route back. The Preview target differs from the wire in one addressing rule: a query-bound repeat's flattened rows carry `@id` / `@index` and have no `item` step. | "No raw-SQL escape hatches", `today` bullet | "The database session timezone cannot change overdue decisions." | Kept; adds "A CSQL slot arrives folded: the day there is the one Core's `today()` gives in the viewer's zone, which is the same day." | 10 |
| same, `datetime-coerce` bullet | "Naive `data_type: "datetime"` literals pin the same way, statically, in `compileLiteral`." | "Naive `data_type: "datetime"` literals pin the same way, statically, in `compileLiteral`, and a custom `datetime` property read pins the same way at run time (`compilePinnedInstant(read, "UTC")`), so a naive stored value never follows the session `TimeZone`." | 7 |
| "Portable case-list column reads" (whole section) | "## Portable case-list column reads" / "Calculated Results/Details values set `TermCompileContext.portableCaseDates`." … "Calendar-only branches and custom datetime values retain their existing shapes." | "## Readings" / "Every compile site states what the rows it reads stand for, in a required `TermCompileContext.reading: CaseReading`: `casedb` (a device evaluating over its casedb: a browse list's filter, calculated columns and sort keys, the filter preview of a module without an effective Search, and case operations); `results` (a device evaluating over HQ's search results instance: the calculated columns and sort keys of an effective Search's results); or `search` (HQ's case search: an effective Search's composed query, the search results snapshot, and every tenant-wide authoring read). Under `casedb`, `date_opened` and `last_modified` read as the date of `timezone(viewerTimeZone, stored_timestamp)`, Core's casedb `DateData`, and against an instant as that date's midnight in the viewer's zone. Under `results`, `date_opened` is the UTC calendar date of `opened_on` (HQ's `json_format_date`) and `last_modified` its instant. Under `search`, both are the stored instants. `caseDataBindingHelpers.ts` chooses the reading from the module's effective Search, through the projection the wire reads, and `SortKey.reading` carries it through ordinary and grouped ordering. It follows self, parent and nested predicate reads." / "Temporal result inference does not revalidate an admitted expression against different property types. It follows value branches independently of conditions. A mixed date/datetime branch, and a `casedb` system date compared with an instant, promote the calendar value to midnight in the validated viewer timezone before PostgreSQL merges or compares them; connection timezone must not decide that instant. No other implicit promotion exists: the checker refuses an uncoerced date against a date and time in every profile. Calendar-only branches and custom datetime values retain their existing shapes." | 7 |
| Readings (the PR 7 section)  | none | Adds "`closed_on` reads its column only under `search`; under any other reading it is an invariant, because the checker admits it only in an advanced search predicate." | 10 |
| New section after "Readings"  | none | Adds "## Datetime comparisons" / "Every comparison of two datetimes compiles as an instant comparison, equality included. A custom `datetime` property and a naive literal pin to UTC; `now` is the transaction instant; `datetime-coerce` of text or a date is UTC midnight, HQ's meaning. A `casedb` system date is the viewer-midnight instant of the `DateData` a device holds, a `results` `date_opened` is the UTC date the results instance holds, and a `search` system date is the stored instant. `__tests__/instantOrdering.postgres.test.ts` holds these verdicts to Core's and Preview's over the shared operand table, in four viewer and session zones chosen apart." | 7 |
| New section after "Datetime comparisons"  | none | Adds "## Two-valued predicates" / "A predicate over a missing value is false, never SQL `NULL`. So `not`, `!=` and `= ''` keep a case that lacks the property, as a device (an absent node unpacks to `''`) and HQ (`!=` and `not` are `NOT` of the match) both keep it. `PredicateCompileContext.polarity` flips under `not` and is restored inside `exists` / `missing`, whose `where` selects rows positively. An atom (a comparison, `match`, `multi-select-contains`, `within-distance`, a lookup row's `matches`) compiles bare in positive polarity, where `NULL` and false both exclude the row and its expression index still applies, and as `coalesce(<atom>, false)` in negative polarity. `!=` is `not` of the `=` atom, and `in` and `between` compose atoms. A comparison of a property with the literal `''` is the absence check whatever the property's type (`= ''` is `is-blank`, `!= ''` its negation). An equality with a bound runtime comparand is the absence check when the comparand is blank and the typed comparison otherwise. `exists`, `missing`, `is-blank`, `match-all` and `match-none` are two-valued already, and `if` in a predicate is `CASE WHEN` with its branches in the enclosing polarity." | 7 |
| New section after "Two-valued predicates"  | none | Adds "## Day numbers" / "Under `casedb` and `results` a day number is Core's, in the viewer's zone `z` (`coreDayNumber.ts`). `date-coerce(n)` is `timezone(z, E + n * interval '24 hours' + interval '12 hours')::date`, with `E = timezone(z, timestamp '1970-01-01 00:00:00')`, and a date read as a number is `floor(extract(epoch from (timezone(z, d::timestamp) - E + interval '12 hours')) / 86400)`, as `DateUtils.daysSinceEpoch` computes it. Under `search` it is HQ's calendar arithmetic, `date '1970-01-01' + n`. The two agree except in the five Pacific zones whose offset moved by more than twelve hours after 1970, where a device's list and HQ's search differ by a day and Preview shows each as its runtime does." | 7 |
| New paragraph after "Day numbers"  | none | Adds "## CSQL slots arrive folded" / "Preview evaluates a Search's runtime segments, runtime choices and obligations in the browser, and `lib/commcare/csql/fold.ts::foldCsqlRuntime` replaces each with a literal, its chosen branch or `match-none` before this compiler runs, so no runtime leaf reaches a CSQL compile. `value-list` compiles to `= any(<array>)`, with `!=` its two-valued negation. A runtime distance unit or radius compiles as a `CASE` over the nine units' meter factors (`lib/domain/expression/distance.ts::METERS_PER_DISTANCE_UNIT`), an unknown unit or a radius not above zero matching nothing. `if` and `switch` over clauses compile as `CASE WHEN`, a null condition taking the else branch. `datetime-coerce` of a number is epoch days as an instant (`timestamptz '1970-01-01 00:00:00+00' + n * interval '24 hours'`)." | 10 |
| "Case-list sort keys" | "Most keys are a `ValueExpression` this compiler already compiles" | "Most keys are an `Expression` this compiler already compiles" | 5 |
| same | (the PR 5 text) | Adds after the first paragraph's first sentence: "Each key carries the list's reading (`SortKey.reading`, "Readings")." | 7 |
| same | "The other key that is no `ValueExpression` is an interval column's shown text" | "The other key that is no `Expression` is an interval column's shown text" | 5 |
| "Numeric expressions" | "Every arithmetic node preserves its grouping, including a nested right operand with the same precedence: `10 - (5 - 2)` must return `7`." | Kept; adds "`div` is real division over `numeric`, `quotient` is `trunc(a / b)` over `numeric`, and `negate` negates its numeric operand." | 5 |
| "Blank semantics" | "`is-blank` matches absent-or-empty, the one absence meaning Nova's Postgres runtime and every CommCare wire target can preserve identically." | Kept; adds "A comparison with the literal `''` compiles as the same check ("Two-valued predicates")." | 7 |

#### lib/session/CLAUDE.md

| Section | Today | From step 3 | PR |
|---|---|---|---|
| "Preview navigation is ephemeral and identity-based", `previewSearchStates` | "It is written by the case-list screen as its query settles and read by the form screen to admit the no-matches registration form." | "It is written by the case-list screen as its query settles and read by the form screen to admit the no-matches registration form, and to load the completed attempt's results snapshot for a menu form that reads what the search found." | 9 |
| same | "Home clears the current task's case facts and search context, without changing worker identity or authorization." | "Home clears the current task's case facts and search context, without changing worker identity, authorization or the worker's claims." | 7 |
| same, new paragraph after the `previewSearchStates` paragraph  | none | Adds "`previewClaims` holds the running worker's claims: an ordered set of case ids, keyed by app and the identity's `ownerId`. Choosing a search result (one case, or each case of a several-case selection) that the worker's restore does not hold adds its id, as HQ's claim adds it to the worker's device. Every device-scoped read of that worker sends the set as plain JSON `claimedCaseIds`. Claims last while the worker, the app's Project and the page stay; Home keeps them, because a claim on HQ outlives the task. A different worker, app or Project starts from none. An App Test journey holds its claims in its own state on the server (`lib/preview/app-tests/CLAUDE.md`), never here, and never reads these. A claimed case is held for an entry-point launch, whose device snapshot sends the claims like every other device-scoped read." (part 07 §3.5) | 7 |

## Public docs that change

Every sentence follows `design/readme.md`, "Content fundamentals": it invites, takes
responsibility, offers the next step, and uses no em dash. No `design/` change: step 3 adds no
visual primitive (part 02 §7). The public docs do not describe the refusal of wording that shows an
answer (`itext-reference`, part 03 §3.7), because no surface offers `jr:itext` (part 02 §4.2); a
comparison of two dates and times that HQ's form builder shows lowered "keeps its meaning if you
leave it as it is", since reading the lowered shape back is step 6's; and the choice paragraph sits
in `building-with-nova.mdx`, "Text in your app", because `case-changes.mdx`'s option paragraph is
about operations.

#### content/docs/building-with-nova.mdx

| Section | Today | From step 3 | PR |
|---|---|---|---|
| "XPath that will run in CommCare", the lookup paragraph | "Lookup-table formulas use Nova's table and column references, which keep their identity when a table tag changes. Raw XPath can use the stable `casedb` and `commcaresession` instances, but it cannot name a lookup table by its mutable wire tag." | "A formula in a form can read a Project data table by its export tag. For example, `instance('facilities')/facilities_list/facilities[code = #form/facility]/name` reads the name of the facility a worker chose. Nova keeps the table and its columns themselves rather than their names, so renaming the table's export tag or a column keeps the formula reading them, and a table or column a formula reads can't be removed until that formula changes. Record expressions use Nova's table and column references, which work the same way." | 9 |
| same, after the lookup paragraph (new) | (none) | adds "A formula can also read your records, for example `count(instance('casedb')/casedb/case[@case_type = 'visit'][@status = 'open'])`. A query that reads a property of its records names their record type with `[@case_type = '...']`. When it leaves the type out, Nova uses the one record type that has that property, and asks you to add the type when none or several do. Because Nova knows each query's record type, renaming a property updates the queries that read it." | 5 |
| same, next (new) | (none) | adds "A formula can name a question by its `#form/` path, by its full `/data/` path, or relative to where the formula runs, such as `../age` for the answer in the same repeat entry. Nova keeps the spelling you chose and follows the question when you rename or move it. A move that would leave a relative path with no way to reach its question is refused, and Nova names the formula that reads it." | 5 |
| same, next (new; part 05 §4 "session values") | (none) | adds "A formula can read values the app's menus supply to a form, such as the ID of the case chosen in a menu or the ID this form's new case will get, through `instance('commcaresession')/session/data/`. Preview supplies them as a device does, so a registration form that reads its new case's ID reads the ID the case is saved with." | 7 |
| same paragraph, appended after "…through `instance('commcaresession')/session/data/`." | (none) | adds "The formula editor lists the values this form receives, each labelled by what it means." | 9 |
| same, opening paragraph "When a formula needs correction" | "When a formula needs correction, your draft and keyboard focus stay in the editor so you can keep typing while its explanation is visible." | kept; adds after it "After you save a formula, it shows in Nova's standard spelling: one space after each comma and on each side of an operator, only the brackets it needs, and single quotes unless the text holds one. The spelling of each question path you chose stays. What the formula does is unchanged, and Nova publishes it in the same spelling." | 5 |
| same, after "Nova never saves a formula that its owning Preview surface…" (new: comparing dates and times, part 04 §7) | "it is not an expected state the author needs to repair." (closing words of the paragraph it follows) | kept; adds "Comparing two dates and times compares the moments they name, on every device and in Preview, so a visit at 9:00 comes before one at 17:00 on the same day. Comparing a date with a date and time is refused, because CommCare compares a date by its day. So is ordering a date and time against a number or text. You can wrap the date and time in `date()` to compare days: for a date-and-time question, `date()` gives the day on the worker's device, and for a date and time saved as text, it keeps the day the text was written with. Checking whether a date and time is blank, with `= ''`, still asks only whether it is blank. On a device, a case's opened date and last-changed date hold only the day, so Nova refuses comparing them with a moment such as `now()`. You can compare their day instead, for example `date(#household/date_opened) = today()`. If you open a form in CommCare HQ's form builder, each comparison of two dates and times shows as a longer formula that compares the moments. It keeps its meaning if you leave it as it is." | 8 |
| same paragraph, last sentence appended | (none) | adds after "…`date(#household/date_opened) = today()`.": "In what a search found, the opened date is its day in UTC." | 9 |
| same, next (new: part 03's other refusals) | (none) | adds "Nova also refuses a few comparisons CommCare would answer in a way you didn't mean, and says what to write instead. Comparing an answer with `true()` or `false()` is refused, because CommCare counts any answer that isn't blank as true, so \"no\" would count too. You can compare it with the choice's value instead, like `#form/consent = 'yes'`. Outside a repeating section, an answer inside it has one value for every entry, so you can count the entries, add them up, join them, or read one entry, like `#form/visits[1]/date`. Using every entry where one value is needed is refused, because the form would stop once there were two entries." | 6 |
| same, the hidden value paragraph "A hidden value has one expression" | "A hidden value with no expression that saves to a case property says under it what happens: it carries the case's current value through unchanged where the form opens that case, and saves the property blank otherwise." | kept; adds after it "A hidden value that saves to a case property gives it the kind of value its expression works out to, and that has to fit the property and its other questions. One that saves text to a property that holds dates is refused, because a form that fills a date question from that property couldn't open. You can change the calculation so it gives a date, or save it to another property. A fixed value saved into a property a choice question starts on has to be one of that question's choices, so the question can open showing it." | 6 |
| "Text in your app", the choice paragraph | "Each choice you write for a select question has a label, or an image, audio or video, so a worker never sees an empty choice." | kept; adds after it "A formula that compares a select question with one of its choices, and a starting value that picks one, keep the choice itself rather than its value. Changing the choice's value updates them, and a choice can't be removed while a formula or a starting value uses it: Nova names each one so you can change it first. If the question's choices move to a Project data table or to cases, those comparisons keep the choice's current value as text, so they still mean the same thing. A fixed starting value has to be one of the question's choices, or empty, because CommCare's Web Apps can't open a form whose question starts on a choice it doesn't offer." | 5 |
| "Try the app in Preview", the answer-check paragraph | "When a form opens an existing record, its questions become available after that record finishes loading. This keeps early answers from being replaced as the form starts." | kept; adds after it "When a form opens a case whose saved value doesn't fit its question, such as text that isn't a date for a date question, CommCare won't open the form for that case, and Preview doesn't either. Preview names the value, and you can correct it in Case data, then open the form again. A starting value that works out to the wrong kind of value stops the form the same way, and Preview names the question so you can change its starting value." | 7 |

`cross-facility-referrals.mdx` (browse lists keep the restore scope), `deep-links.mdx` ("Preview
does not claim or sync cases from CommCare HQ." stays true: Preview's claim never reaches HQ),

and every page under `claude-code/` and `mcp/` but `tools.mdx`.
