| finding 75 | (none) | "75. **A case list filter or a search that asks for a date and time equal to a value finds nothing on CommCare, and Preview finds the cases.** Nova writes every search comparison as `<property> <op> <value>` whatever the property's type (`lib/commcare/predicate/csqlEmitter.ts::emitComparisonSegments`), and a literal as written (`lib/commcare/predicate/termEmitter.ts::emitCsqlLiteralSegment`). HQ's receiver rewrites every date-and-time-shaped string of a submission before saving it (`form_processor/parsers/form.py::_create_new_xform`, `form_processor/utils/xform.py::adjust_datetimes`) as the UTC instant with six fraction digits (`dimagi/utils/parsing.py::json_format_datetime`: `2026-01-02T04:30:00.000000Z`), whichever writer submitted it (Nova's form, HQ's case API, HQ's case importer), so the case, its search index entry and a device's restore all hold that spelling. HQ's search compares a custom property with `=` as text (`case_search/xpath_functions/comparison.py::property_comparison_query`, `es/case_search.py::case_property_query`, an exact term on `case_properties.value.exact`), and `!=` is its `NOT`; an ordering parses the value and compares instants (`case_property_range_query`). Executed against HQ at the pin, with cases saved through HQ's receiver in twelve spellings of one instant: `= '2026-01-02T09:30:00.000+05'` finds none of them, `= '2026-01-02T04:30:00.000000Z'` finds all twelve, and every ordering finds by instant. HQ's `now()` and `datetime()` print `isoformat()` (`value_functions.py::now`, `::datetime_`) and a value Core prints carries the device's offset, so nothing Nova sends equals the stored text. On a system date HQ sends a date-and-time value as a raw term (`_create_system_datetime_query`), which Elasticsearch parses against the index's date formats (`corehq/pillows/core.py::DATE_FORMATS_STRING`), each of which with a time requires seconds, so the minute-precision value the builder's picker writes makes the search fail: executed with every reader, HQ answers `date_opened = '2026-01-02T09:30'` with a 500 (`ESError`, Elasticsearch's `RequestError(400)`, "failed to parse date field [2026-01-02T09:30]"), Formplayer answers with its internal-server-error notification, the Web Apps client shows HQ's error alert, and Android "Server-side error (code 500)". The device's case list filter compares two strings by text too (commcare-core `XPathEqExpr.java::testEquality`). Preview compiles the same filter in Postgres, which casts the property to `timestamptz` (`lib/case-store/sql/dataTypeTokens.ts::POSTGRES_CAST_FOR_DATA_TYPE`) and compares instants. *Harm:* a worker's list or search for visits at a given time shows none on the device or on HQ while Preview shows them; `!=` shows every case; on a system date the search fails. *Documents:* `targeted-datetime-equality` (intent, `/caseList/rows/*/caseId` on the local export: "Visits" filters `seen` equal to a date and time and Core lists none of the one case Nova's meaning lists; "Other visits" filters it not equal and Core lists every case; with every reader, "Opened visits" searches `date_opened` equal to a minute-precision date and time, HQ answers 500, Formplayer's walk stops (`proof/walk-stops.json`), and proofs 3 and 4 hold Formplayer's answer at `/hq/app_aware_remote_search/500/corehq.apps.es.exceptions.ESError` and `/runs/*/steps/*/response/exception`)." | 1 |

Its two entries are in the fixed-defect register, held on the control `targeted-datetime-equality`." | 8 |
| finding 75 | (PR 8 text) | adds "*Fixed (#<PR 10>):* a search's `=`, `!=` and `in` on a date and time are a closed instant range (`(p >= v and p <= v)`, `lib/commcare/csql/emitter.ts`), which HQ's range query reads in every spelling, and a date-and-time literal is held to milliseconds (`datetime-literal-precision`), the resolution HQ's index keeps. The `csql` native family's `datetime-equality` scenario holds it: HQ's case search over cases its receiver saved returns the expected ids, as Postgres does. Its four search entries are in the fixed-defect register and its walk stop is held on the control `targeted-datetime-equality`, which keeps the search from before the fix." | 10 |

## Written by this plan's own pull request

This plan's own pull request, the one that adds `3-expressions.md` and its parts, writes the
deferral notes into the outline files of the steps that receive them, so nothing step 3 leaves is
dropped silently and every later step reads what it inherits before step 3's code lands. Each note
says what the receiving step gets, why step 3 left it (a model concept a later step owns, or the
platform declaration), and what step 3 does meanwhile. A construct leaves step 3 only for one of
those two reasons (decided with the person, 2026-10-17); the list below is every one, the top
file's "Deferrals only with a reason" in full. What Nova does not author because it is an escape
hatch of HQ's own editors is no deferral: it is held in the union so an import keeps it, and the
`6-import.md` note says so with the reason.

Step 3 refuses it outside the no-matches form (`INVALID_SEARCH_REF`), which keeps its `#search/` read. | "What it builds", after item 7 | "7. **Feature-matrix apps.** At least one HQ app per manifest entry, built through HQ's own models, plus generated combinations. This step builds them, because the reader is the first consumer of HQ-authored apps; the proof lane's corpus holds none before it." | adds "8. **What step 3 left to import.** Each is a shape only an imported app holds, so no step-3 surface authors it:\n   - *Project-timezone days.* HQ compares a date with `date_opened`, `closed_on` or `last_modified` as the project's day (`case_search/xpath_functions/comparison.py::_create_system_datetime_query`, `adjust_input_date_by_timezone`, `Domain.default_timezone`). No route an API key opens returns a project space's timezone, so no step-3 surface can author it with a meaning Preview evaluates as HQ does; Nova's search keeps the worker's day for `today()` and the UTC day for a date coerced to a date and time. This step decides how an imported comparison of that shape is held and how Preview evaluates it; `case-search/date-only-comparison-on-date-opened-closed-on-last-modified` stays HELD-NEW until then.\n   - *Several `_xpath_query` rows.* HQ ANDs every row (`case_search/utils.py::_apply_filter`), so one row or several find the same cases, and Nova's own searches have no row identity to keep. An imported list of rows, each a typed default filter emitted as its own row, needs a slot that holds a list, which this step adds with where an imported row lives (a row may read what no device holds, such as `closed_on`, so it is not the case list filter). `case-search/several-xpath-query-rows` stays HELD-NEW until then.\n   - *Arbitrary `<output value>` expressions in prose, and prose holding `instance('…')`.* Prose keeps its stored shape in step 3: its four reference part kinds hold identity (`field-ref`, `case-ref`, `user-property-ref` and the external `user-ref`, `lib/domain/prose.ts`), and changing the shape would re-fingerprint every translation. HQ's form builder admits any expression in an `<output value>`, and HQ's `add_missing_instances` scans the whole rendered XForm, labels included (`xform.py::XForm.add_missing_instances`, `post_process/instances.py::get_instance_names`), so a label whose text holds `instance('x')` makes HQ add or refuse an instance. Both are about which text in a form's prose CommCare reads as XPath, and only an imported app holds either; this step decides how an imported label's output expressions and its `instance(…)` text are stored and printed.\n   - *HQ's escape hatches, which Nova holds and does not author.* HQ's own editors offer a list known only at run time (`unwrap-list('<json>')`), `casedb`, lookup-row and location reads inside a record CSQL value other than the chosen case's property, and a function call in a record slot. Nova's authoring has its own arms for what a person builds (a fixed list is `in`; a calculated column has its arms), so it does not reproduce an escape hatch because HQ has one. The union holds `value-list` and those reads, and the checker, the emitter, Preview's fold and Postgres handle them, natively proven, so the reader reads `unwrap-list` into `value-list` and each read into its arm, and `case-search/unwrap-list` and `case-search/runtime-values-read-from-casedb-or-session-datums` are HELD from step 3. `call` is a form-logic arm in step 3; this step admits it in record slots for import and owns compiling to Postgres the functions an imported calculated column uses (`substr`, `round`, `int`, `join`, `lower-case` and the others HQ's calculated columns reach for), since only an imported column needs one and Postgres compiles none of `call`'s functions in step 3.\n   - *The lowered date-and-time comparison.* From step 3 Nova writes a comparison of two dates and times inline as zone-free instant arithmetic (`lib/commcare/xpath/wire/instant.ts`), in the form and in its Vellum shadow, so HQ's form builder shows the lowered formula and keeps it on save. The reader recognises the shape and reads it back as the comparison the author wrote: the template with one operand subtree `T` repeated, carrying the constants `719468`, `146097`, `'Z+-'` and `'\|\|\|'`; `T` is the operand, or `format-date(coalesce(x, ''), '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%3%Z')` around a typed operand `x`, or `concat(<date>, 'T00:00:00Z')` around a coerced date; an integer opposite a lowered operand is a folded literal, read back as its `…Z` spelling. Nothing in step 3 reads lowered text back." (The pipes are escaped for this table only.) | this plan's pull request |

## The nova-plugin check

`../nova-plugin` (read-only for this part, at `14af38e`, "Document HQ round trip publishing
(v2.2.0) (#70)", `version` `2.2.0` in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`) was swept over every prose file
it tracks: `README.md`, `agents/nova-architect-autonomous.md` and the seven `skills/*/SKILL.md`
(`.dev-plugin/` is a generated copy and is not edited). The sweep was by claim, for every claim step
3 changes: XPath and form-logic spellings, hashtags, case queries and their record type, relative
paths, data-table reads in forms, places, session values, search results, the catalog's required
and validation rules, which retire, record expressions (`record-type()`, `here()`, distance units, Search's
`today()`), renames, and what a read returns (canonical spelling). Terms searched: xpath, hashtag,
`#form`, `#case`, `#user`, casedb, `instance(`, expression, formula, condition, calculat, validation,
required, record type, case type, lookup, relative, rename, preserv, identit, reference, query,
filter, search, today, distance, miles, kilomet, default, choice, option, date.

What the plugin says, and what step 3 does to it:

| File, passage | Today | From step 3 |
|---|---|---|
| `README.md`, "Authoring guidance" | "Agents write wording and expressions as text; Nova resolves references and preserves stable identities when content is renamed." | Stays, and becomes true where it was not: today a relative path or a case query held as text strands on a rename (defect 17, part 02 §1.1); from pull request 5 every reference is an identity. No edit |
| `README.md`, the operation-feedback paragraph; `skills/build/SKILL.md` and `skills/edit/SKILL.md`, the `operationSemantics` sentence | "Form and case-operation feedback includes record targets and condition/value dependencies."; "`operationSemantics`: record targets, condition/value dependencies and possible read/write overlaps." | Stays true: the walk now reports a queried record's read by record type instead of an unclassified-text reason (part 02 §3.4), which is a narrower case of "condition/value dependencies". No edit |
| `skills/build/SKILL.md`, `skills/edit/SKILL.md`, `agents/nova-architect-autonomous.md`, the case-backed choices paragraph | "Case-backed choices offer records already available to the worker and save exact record IDs. Filters do not fetch additional cases or broaden access" | Stays true: a cases-sourced select still reads the device's cases; only a search-first module's results read beyond the restore (part 07 §3.5), and no plugin sentence speaks of search results. No edit |
| `skills/upload_to_hq/SKILL.md`, description | "… along with the lookup tables it reads and the places in its organization." | Stays true: form-logic table reads join the same reference extractors that decide which tables a publish pushes (part 05 §2.3, part 02 §5.5). No edit |
| `README.md`, the app-test paragraph | "After editing and saving an app calculation, start a fresh test so it exercises the new saved checkpoint." | Stays true. `RUNTIME_VERSION` 16 refuses a test stored before the deploy with Nova's own message ("Nova's test runtime changed. Start a new test."), which the tool returns. No edit |
| `skills/show/SKILL.md`, `skills/edit/SKILL.md`, the notices sentence | "If `notices` is non-empty, tell the user what each one says before anything else …" | Covers the step 3 notice (cutover `hq-round-trip-expressions`) with no change: the skills relay each notice's text, whatever its reasons. No edit |

No skill teaches an expression spelling: `build`, `autobuild`, `edit` and the autonomous agent send
the model to `get_agent_prompt` and `get_authoring_guide`, which Nova serves and which change with the
deploy (pull requests 9, 10 and 12). The claims parts 02 §3.8, 05 §4 and 06 §7 named for the sweep
("raw XPath cannot read a table", "miles or kilometers", "Search compares a case property", a
catalog validation rule) appear nowhere in the plugin, and neither does a claim about what a record rule can read after a case is chosen (`grep -n -i` for chosen, parent case and selected case finds one unrelated hit, the target project space in `skills/upload_to_hq/SKILL.md`). So at `14af38e` step 3 makes no plugin
sentence false, and the plugin needs no change.

The rule for the stack, so a sentence added to the plugin after `14af38e` is caught:

1. Pull request 12, which ships the last model-facing text, runs the sweep again at the plugin's
   head then, by claim and never by this list of files, against the guide text it ships.
2. If that sweep finds a sentence step 3 makes false, the fix is one pull request in `../nova-plugin`,
   cut after pull request 12 and merged only after the Nova deploy is live (house rule 10 confirms
   it). Its `version` in `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` goes to the next patch from whatever it holds
   when that pull request is cut (`2.2.0` makes `2.2.1`: corrected guidance, no new command), and
   `README.md` gains one sentence beside "Version 2.2's publishing guidance …": that release's
   authoring guidance requires the paired Nova server release, the one this stack deploys. If it
   finds none, the plugin is untouched and `plugin.json` does not move, as part 02 §3.8 decided.

the first nightly; none needs a third run of anything.

   `Expression` union; `lib/domain/predicate` and `lib/domain/xpath` are gone; the commit gate makes
   no Lezer parse; `parse(print(e)) = e` holds per grammar in the round-trip suites and over every
   lifted production expression (part 09 §10).

   or 75; `proof/fixed-defects.json` 734, finding 56's seven, finding 75's six and defect 25's one

   its control (`targeted-datetime-ordering`, `targeted-datetime-operands`,

   `test_every_live_defect_is_a_later_steps` asserts `step > 3`; `proof/identity-moves.json` empty;
   `RULES` 25.
3. **The walk stops at exit**: `proof/walk-stops.json` holds 25, the 29 of the base less findings 72's
   five and 73's one, with no control kept for either, plus finding 75's search stop, held on the

   `fuzz-xform-20260930-0`. Finding 74's two stops, that and the unchanged
   `targeted-datetime-ordering` stop, stay for step 4; no stop on the document
   `targeted-datetime-equality`. A stop the first nightly shows on a document step 3 added is either a reader's own end, added with its cause, or a Nova defect, made a
   finding with a targeted document.
4. **Finding 56 is fixed**: a comparison of two dates and times orders and compares by instant on
   every device, in every zone of the `expression` family's seven setups, and in Preview
   (`instantLowering.test.ts`); its seven requirements each hold at their named test (part 08 §2.3).
5. **Finding 75 is fixed**: its device half by the equality lowering (its two entries in the fixed
   register), its HQ half by the closed instant range, which the `csql` family's `datetime-equality`
   scenario holds over cases saved through HQ's receiver, and which ends the search error on a
   system date (its four entries in the fixed register, its stop on the control).
6. **Finding 72's and 73's stops are gone**: `SELECT_DEFAULT_NOT_AN_OPTION` refuses a literal choice
   default outside its options (`selectDefault.test.ts`), Hidden Values join writer agreement
   (`hiddenValueWriterAgreement.test.ts`), the lane seeds each property from the effective catalog
   (`test_the_case_database_seeds_each_property_from_the_effective_catalog`), and Preview refuses a
   preload that does not cast, as Core does.
7. **Defect 17 is fixed**: a rename or move reaches every reference by identity, relative paths and
   case queries included (`gate.test.ts`'s relative-read rename; `targeted-expression-renames` under
   proof 5).
8. **Every existing app is valid and has its notice.** The cutover committed (the execute run's
   report, archived to `gs://commcare-nova-cutover-evidence/hq-round-trip-expressions/`); every app
   loads under the new schema and gate with `exportReadinessFindings` empty; every app with a planned

9. **The first nightly with every reader holds**, or each group it names failing is fixed and rerun

10. **The served contract is checked**: `MODEL_CONTEXT_VERSION` is
    `"v4-hq-round-trip-expressions.12"`; the billed schema check passed for every tool it named, or
